  "findings": [
    {
      "title": "One retro clears the whole retro debt",
      "body": "The person: \"doing one retro needs to clear the retro debt overall.\" Collect lists every other open retro or overhaul issue of the project and every work finding of earlier retros that no issue names; `retro.py file` closes the open ones as covered by its report, linking it, and the report carries the unfiled findings forward. Today: a hand-filed retro stayed open 19 days and #864's 12 findings were never named.",
      "class": "Retro debt carried forward",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "It changes how a retro closes: one run now settles every open retro and carries earlier findings, the person's new rule. Decided by the person, 24 Nov: bless."
    },
    {
      "title": "Next filing waits for retro findings",
      "body": "The first `work_tickets.py file` after a retro closes refuses until each of its work findings is named by an issue (`retro #<n> finding <k>`) or recorded as declined, naming the missing ones. #864's findings went unfiled and the next collect could score none.",
      "class": "Retro debt carried forward",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Mirror sync becomes one command",
      "body": "The person: \"you're not auditing; you're just merging.\" A sync of a mirror from the public line took about 20 minutes as an audit. Promote the machine-local sync into SE: report each repository's state, apply where no overlay exists (one squashed sync commit, landing, tree parity), stop only where files must be replayed. It reuses `work_tickets.py parity` and the landing step; no audit, no broad suite.",
      "class": "Routine operations run as audits",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Report source, binary, core, host versions",
      "body": "The person's comment: after an update MCP still asked for binary 0.14.0 while SE 13.1.0 and binary 1.1.0 were installed. The start report names the checkout, the binary, the running core and the host's loaded agent and MCP version apart, and says when the host must reload; a refreshed checkout is never reported as a restarted runtime.",
      "class": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "home": "scripts/se_start.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Clear or compact reloads runtime and guidance",
      "class": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "home": "scripts/se_start.py",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "Decided by the person, 24 Nov: the self-restart design is declined and replaced by this.",
      "body": "The person asked for a reset after an update; the recommended self-restart through the host's session tool is declined. Instead a clear or a compact of the session reloads what an update changed: the agent definition, the `se` MCP server's binary and the guidance, through the start hook that runs on both. The start report (finding 4) says when a clear is needed. Evidence over the window: cloud sessions ran an older SE plugin, a stale core (pid 255) or stale runtime records than their checkout (#1030, #1036, #1064, #1068, #1072, #1081, #1101, #1625, #1674, #1786, #1790)."
    },
    {
      "title": "Refuse raw issue creation in SE sessions",
      "body": "24 Nov: the SE session filed #1811 with raw `gh issue create` while retro #1810 held the gate. A PreToolUse hook in SE's plugin refuses `gh issue create` (and the host's issue-create tool) in a checkout with SE, naming `work_tickets.py file`.",
      "class": "Filing gate bypassed by hand",
      "home": "scripts/se_hook.sh",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "It takes a tool away from every SE session, trading flexibility for the gate holding; an outside repository's filing must still pass. Decided by the person, 24 Nov: bless; hooks and doors hold it."
    },
    {
      "title": "Guard line names the behaviour",
      "body": "The greeting printed `[copilot] start_sessions = true: off, today's behaviour`; the SE session read it as \"starting sessions is off\" and ran this retro inline. `copilot_guards.state()` names the behaviour: \"SE starts sessions itself (`create_session`)\" or \"SE prints the line, the person starts it\".",
      "class": "Guard line read as its opposite",
      "home": "scripts/copilot_guards.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "se.md states the session default",
      "body": "`src/agents/se.md` says \"You start no session yourself ... unless\" the guard line reads true, the inverse of the built-in default `docs/copilot.md` owns. It states: SE starts worker, integrator, reviewer and retro sessions with the host's session tool, unless `[copilot] start_sessions = false`, and links docs/copilot.md for the setting.",
      "class": "Guard line read as its opposite",
      "home": "src/agents/se.md",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "System code moves to Go on kern",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "docs/go-core.md",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "Decided by the person, 24 Nov: bless, with the stronger direction that system code moves to Go.",
      "body": "The person proposed one Go \"agent tools\" component with subcomponents presented through MCP, and blessed the direction: what is part of the system moves to Go, as components on kern served through the `se` MCP server, with kern's streaming and side-by-side calls; Python stays for ad hoc work (python-ad-hoc-go-system). The order follows the evidence: the commands this window shows as repeated and costly first (collect, the GitHub reads behind deliver and intake, the suite, the mirror sync). It recurred: slow commands without updates came up on 6 Nov (feedback within a second, `feedback.INTERACTIVE` only) and in #864 finding 11 (the window's start, not what collect reads), and neither moved the commands themselves."
    },
    {
      "title": "Collect reads only the window, in parallel",
      "body": "The person, 24 Nov: \"why does 'collect retro draft' take 6 minutes? this shouldnt be an ad-hoc script. everything the agent needs to do needs to be well-prepared\" and \"obviously only collect over the window. how was this ever constructed otherwise, is that not obvious?\" A design defect: `retro.py` `pages()` (line 291) pages through every closed issue and every closed pull request of the repository's whole history, one request after another, and drops those outside the window client-side; the backlog-file due check reads the same way. Why it was built so: the first collect (12 Nov) was written against fake doors whose fixtures hold a handful of issues, where reading everything costs nothing and is simplest to test; the window's start was then fixed (#864 finding 11) without asking what is read. Why no review caught it: tests and reviews checked what collect returns, never how many requests it makes, and no command's cost against the window's size is measured. Fix: ask GitHub for the window only (closed since its start, server-side), read the per-issue comments and checks in bounded parallel with ordered writes, and hold it with a test that counts the requests a window of n issues makes; the same for the backlog-file due check.",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Long commands report progress throughout",
      "body": "The person, 24 Nov: \"no time limit. but updates?\" and earlier \"scripts and tools that take a long time: can they run parallel, and they always need to give status updates\". No time limit is set or enforced. Every long-running command reports, on the CLI and through the `se` MCP server's stream where the host shows it: its phase, done/total where known, elapsed time, and a stall or failure line when nothing advances; independent reads run in parallel with bounded concurrency and dependent writes keep their order. Asked before in #115, #981 and #986.",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "scripts/se_components.py",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "It sets a rule every command must meet and trades output volume for visibility; the interval is the person's. Decided by the person, 24 Nov: bless."
    },
    {
      "title": "Collect counts machine-local script runs",
      "body": "\"Ad hoc scripts to promote\" read None, while the person runs a machine-local sync script often. Collect counts runs of untracked scripts from the session record (left out by #990) and lists those run three or more times as evidence for the judge, not as an automatic port.",
      "class": "Long commands run silent and serial",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Rule check skips release landings",
      "body": "The `work-issue` check counted #1729, #1783, #1791, #1798 and #1807, all release landings, which name no work issue by design. It skips landings `release` makes.",
      "class": "Rule checks count release landings",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Ephemeral list reports may exceed the size cap",
      "body": "Decided by the person, 24 Nov: \"for retro, we can make an exemption here. Normally, retros aren't that big, but if it is big, then we still need to be able to run it. We can't throw stuff out of the retro. In general, listicles?big reports that just list a lot of stuff?can go over the cap, especially if they are ephemeral. If something stays in the documentation forever as it is, then it needs to be cleaned up by the one-time report. One that's just very big can be acceptable.\" The rule: ephemeral list reports (retro docs, collect output, reports) may exceed the cap; permanent documentation stays under it and is cleaned up. Home: the `large` check of `scripts/hygiene.py` (`MAX_BYTES = 200_000`, `LARGE_ALLOWED`), which refuses this doc at about 370 KB; Its fix is #1812: the `large` check skips `docs/retro-*.md`, every other file stays at 200 KB. Nothing was cut from this retro to fit.",
      "class": "Retro doc outgrows the size limit",
      "home": "scripts/hygiene.py",
      "controversial": true,
      "why": "The person decided it in this session; it changes what the size rule protects."
    },
    {
      "title": "Relay places kern before it imports it",
      "body": "`intake` and other relay commands failed with `No module named 'kern'` in 18 steps across chapters 17 to 20 (#1620, #1621, #1622, #1624, #1636, #1647, #1667, #1689, #1690, #1672, #1673, #1674, #1679, #1677, #1723, #1757, #1758, #1773) until `se_modules.py sync kern` ran by hand. `scripts/se_relay.py` line 189 imports `kern.nats` unguarded; the relay places kern at its pin first, as the pytest run does, or refuses naming that command.",
      "class": "Cloud intake lacks kern",
      "home": "scripts/se_relay.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Retro prepares its steps without the person",
      "class": "Retro debt carried forward",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false,
      "body": "The person, 24 Nov: \"I feel the retro can start parts of its work (everything that doesnt rely on user feedback) by itself as an issue that is triggered by the SE agent or an integrator. it cant finish without a human (at least in general, we might allow that later), but it can prepare a lot.\" When a retro falls due, SE or the integrator starts the retro agent, which runs steps 2 to 5 (collect, judge, the judged draft, the deck) on its own; only step 1 (field feedback) and step 6 (decisions) wait for the person. Homes: the retro-due trigger in `scripts/work_tickets.py` and `agents/retro.md`'s procedure."
    },
    {
      "title": "SE steers a running agent, never queues",
      "class": "Messages queue behind a running agent",
      "home": "src/skills/work-model/SKILL.md",
      "controversial": false,
      "body": "24 Nov: three messages SE queued to this running retro agent sat unread for over 10 minutes until SE cancelled them and steered one message instead. The person: \"if you send something, it can't stay queued for 10 minutes.\" Fix: the work model's host procedure under Copilot says SE sends to a running agent with delivery steer, and queues only to an idle one."
    },
    {
      "title": "Collect reads the older Retro bullets",
      "body": "244 of 250 field reports in the window carry `## Retro` as three bullets, \"Went well\", \"Cost time\" and \"Proposed improvement\"; collect parses only the 13 standing-question headings, which 8 reports (chapters 19 and 20) carry, so the timeline and the matrix of chapters 1 to 18 came out empty and the person caught it. Collect reads both forms into the step's cells and the chapter's matrix, and a test fails a collect that leaves a cell empty where the report has an answer.",
      "class": "Collect misses the record it has",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Collect writes no local path",
      "body": "Collect wrote the absolute path of the machine's metrics file into the draft (`retro.py` line 1483, `metrics.path()`), and the public check refused the retro doc until it was replaced by hand with `~/.se/metrics.jsonl`. Collect writes the path home-relative.",
      "class": "Collect misses the record it has",
      "home": "scripts/retro.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Focused tests follow the changed files",
      "body": "The most frequent cost of the window, about 45 steps in 12 chapters: a refusal the focused tests did not run showed only in the complete suite, CI or landing: the docs paragraph lint (#941, #1278, #1290, #1347, #1404, #1405, #1445), the agent definitions, generated goldens and registry tests (#1447, #1448, #1508, #1510, #1513, #1515, #1567, #1572), guard tests outside the design-owner list (#1303, #1304, #1339, #1363), the Go suite for `templates/` (#1629, #1678), hygiene only in CI (#1751). AGENTS.md lists some by hand. A command maps each changed path to the tests and checks that hold it and runs them; the worker runs it before delivering.",
      "class": "Checks found only in the complete suite",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Cloud workers reach GitHub through the relay",
      "body": "About 33 steps in 7 chapters: without a signed-in gh, workers copied issue JSON by hand for `field-report --draft` and `intake`, opened pull requests and posted descriptions by hand, and refreshed issues by hand (#752, #758, #960, #1106, #1114, #1140, #1164, #1189, #1190, #1191, #1204, #1301, #1328, #1334, #1336, #1340, #1342, #1343, #1344, #1345, #1433, #1436, #1620, #1622, #1627, #1636, #1723, #1751, #1752, #1757). `deliver`, `refresh` and `field-report --draft` hand their GitHub reads and writes to kern's relay as `intake` already does, and `--draft` drafts from intake's saved answer.",
      "class": "Cloud workers without gh",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Refusals name the exact command line",
      "body": "About 30 steps in 9 chapters lost time on command shapes the procedure did not match: `--worktree` before the subcommand or missing (#1101, #1278, #1293, #1419), `format` without `--worktree` although worker.md says every command takes it (#1142, #1177, #1190, #1204, #1214, #1244, #1245), `field-report --draft` misread or refusing a planned piece's branch (#1053, #1058, #1075, #1347, #1406, #1418, #1421, #1534, #1546, #1554), bare `[Errno 2]` and `[Errno 17]` errors (#1534), briefs whose command lines the parser refuses (#1565, #1582, #1587, #1588). Every command takes `--worktree` in any position; a refusal prints the command line that works; the briefs' command lines come from the parser, not from prose.",
      "class": "Command lines differ from the procedure",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Push makes a cloud clone whole and current",
      "body": "The first push from a shallow cloud clone was refused (#1302, #1305, #1310, #1324, #1333, #1334), and a stale local `main` gave wrong diffs, a wrong base or dropped work (#942, #983, #1021, #1023, #1032, #1617, #1620). `work_tickets.py push` runs `git fetch --unshallow` where the clone is shallow, and `sync` refreshes the integration base before every diff.",
      "class": "Cloud clones start stale",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Suite comparison measures its base apart",
      "body": "Workers measured before and after by hand, sometimes in the checkout they were editing (#1000, #1162, #1165, #1176, #1259), and performance packages could not judge noisy timings (#1439, #1440, #1441, #1502); the suite crept past its target without action (#1120, #1256). The suite's compare runs the base in its own worktree, repeats a noisy measure, and reports the delta with its spread.",
      "class": "Suite measured by hand",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Filing checks what an issue names",
      "body": "Work issues named doc sections, flags, files or switches that did not hold on main (#1247, #1271, #1290, #1368, #1419, #1506, #1507, #1513, #1514, #1515), so workers spent the start of the package finding out. The filing check resolves every named section, flag and file against the tree, as it already does for script subcommands, and refuses the ones that do not hold.",
      "class": "Work issues name what does not hold",
      "home": "scripts/references.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Integrate flags overlapping open pull requests",
      "body": "Parallel pull requests that touched the same files stopped or held integration (#1568, #1571, #1579, #1588 twice). Before starting workers and before integrating, a check lists open pull requests whose changed files overlap, so SE orders them or the integrator rebases once.",
      "class": "Parallel deliveries collide",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "controversial": false
    },
    {
      "title": "Cloud start makes the session ready",
      "body": "Restored cloud containers started unready: leftover cache folders blocked the suite (#1633, #1677, #1678, #1683), a stale core record (pid 255) read as running (#1081, #1101, #1106), the docs style had to be installed by hand (#1757), and the plugin was older than the checkout (#1625, #1674, #1786). The start command clears what a restored boot left, installs what the checkout pins, and refuses to report ready otherwise.",
      "class": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "home": "scripts/se_start.py",
      "controversial": false
    }

  "classes": [
    {
      "name": "Retro debt carried forward",

      "home": "scripts/retro.py",

    },
    {
      "name": "Routine operations run as audits",
      "cause": "the mirror sync lives in a machine-local script, so an agent improvises an audit",
      "fix": "one SE command runs the sync in phases and stops only to replay",
      "home": "scripts/work_tickets.py",
      "home_kind": "command"
    },
    {
      "name": "Host state lags the checkout",
      "cause": "an update changes files but not the host's loaded agent and MCP state, and one version is reported",
      "fix": "the start report names each version and says when to clear; a clear or compact reloads agent, MCP and guidance; the cloud start makes the session ready",
      "home": "scripts/se_start.py",
      "home_kind": "check"
    },
    {
      "name": "Filing gate bypassed by hand",
      "cause": "the open-retro gate lives only in SE's filing command",

      "home_kind": "refusal"
    },
    {
      "name": "Guard line read as its opposite",
      "cause": "the greeting prints the guard's state, not the behaviour, and se.md states the default inversely",
      "fix": "the line names the behaviour and se.md states the default",
      "home": "scripts/copilot_guards.py",
      "home_kind": "prefill"
    },
    {
